# Week of 2026-11-08 (THEO, home X1-PT36; next reset ~2026-11-15 13:00 UTC)

Format: see README.md. Previous week: 2026-11-01.md (297.7M wallet, 461M trading net). Opened 2026-11-10 at the operator's request; days before that are thinner. Read this first each session, then append.

## Standing rules (operator)

- Use only the Tailwinds MCP tools (`mcp__Tailwinds__stcommand_*`) for every fleet read and write; never the lowercase `stcommand` server (suspended, 503).
- Approvals are the operator's decision. Leave fuel warnings out of updates unless a ship is truly stranded.
- **Buy a ship at the yard nearest where it will trade, not the cheapest** (see `CLAUDE.md`, "Buying a ship"; written 2026-11-10).
- **Trade volume is the growth lever, but only repeatable volume into freshly covered systems during the ~2h premium** — not
  just more units on stale routes.
- Intermediate hull is the **light hauler (~315k)** over 82k shuttles. **Heavy freighter ~2.55M / bulk ~3.29M once cash is around
  10M**, then start selling the small ships.
- **Cash discipline:** buy no hull (light haulers included) until the wallet holds **3M**; keepers wait for **5M**. Escape
  clause: if we are *struggling to get there* (cash flat/falling ~2h, rate under ~80k/h, or idle traders), break the rule and
  buy a hauler to restart growth.
- Opening v3 runs by itself (saved template "Opening: auto at reset", tasks o3-*-1108): THEO-1 tours then trades; 200k keepers on the
  shipyards; 200k contract shuttle; 1M keepers on the top markets; 1M light hauler on the gate crew with gate buying; FAB_MATS ceiling
  2,000, ADVANCED_CIRCUITRY ceiling 4,000. Adjust in real time (operator, 2026-11-08).

- 2026-11-10 13:55 **Operator: release the home system and find out why it was queued ("it shouldn't have been").** Done: `release_system X1-PT36` at about 13:30 (ok, markets D49, no keepers unparked); read back: dispatcher routes 6 to 24, THEO-16 assigned COPPER_ORE contractBuy at PT36-J62 (rejectedCount 23 there, to look at), THEO-1E and THEO-7 working AR37 routes. Cause (read from the code): at 12:57:14 the log shows "THEO-31 jump: X1-PT36: skipped 6 thin market(s)", i.e. a keeper probe jumping through the home hub toward BY84. With `remoteSurveyIntake` on, `FleetManager.judgeAndIntake()` (fleet.ts, called after every fleet jump) judged X1-PT36 like a newly surveyed system; its only guard was "already in the queue"; the home system is not in the queue (it is live from the start), passed the worth test, and `intakeKeeperCandidates` queued it with the one market that was not on the keeper priority list (D49), as scouted. Queued and not live means gated: all its routes were held, route count 43 to 6, no sale from 12:50 to 13:24. Fix written (not yet deployed): pure `systemAlreadyLive()` in keeperBuy.ts (true for the home system, or any system with a keeper already on one of its markets), used as an early return in `judgeAndIntake()` and `intakeKeeperCandidates()`; 3 new tests, tsc clean; CHANGELOG entry added. Until the box deploys main, another keeper jump through the hub could queue it again: watch `ops system_queue` for X1-PT36 "scouted", and the hourly update checks it. Cost of the stall: about 33 minutes with no sales (baseline about 50-60k an hour) plus idle traders. Still open: THEO-16's rejectedCount 23 at PT36 (23 routes rejected for it), and the 13:12 update that fired at 13:21.

  106,145. Cash 173,239 at 21:11 → 523,511 at ~22:00 → 208,523 after the hauler buy. 429s 0, queue 0, API 7–21% of the 105 cap.

  repeats of 2026-11-01 lessons:
  1. **Over-bought keepers.** 39 keepers + 14 parked = 53 intel hulls vs 9 traders. Last week's gate lesson: "one touring
     shuttle instead of early keepers, keepers only on the gate's producers and their inputs." ~1.5M of probes is capital that
     should have been haulers — the post-gate rate is 112k/h, capacity-bound.
  2. **Released X1-DJ26 + X1-FR76 together and bought keepers.** Last week: "staging several systems at once costs about 490k an
     hour in probe jumps; stage one at a time closer to the keeper yards." This week positioningCosts 156,098/24h (4.45%), and
     21 jumps / 156,799c in 80 min.
  3. **Thin-market big buys.** THEO-21 bought 62u MEDICINE for 296,112 (and 63u for 283,059) into a tv-100 market — the buy-side
     of the thin-market lesson. Tied up ~300k per load for a thin margin. Capped to lotSize 20 × maxLots 2.
  4. **Manual cross-system routes cost ~100k in jumps in 80 min** (THEO-44 46k, THEO-1E 27k, THEO-1 14k, THEO-21 13k). Cleared.
- **Action:** cleared THEO-1E and THEO-44 routes; capped THEO-21 lots; `manual routes remaining: []`.
- **Plan to lift the rate:** freeze keeper/probe purchases (33 priority markets uncovered — not the bottleneck); put every spare
  credit into light haulers (~315k, ~40k/h, ~8h payback); keep traders on the high-per-unit systems (PT36 last bucket 558,
  BY84 726, FR76 294).
- **Open:** rate 112k/h vs the 2026-11-01 baseline of 1.4-1.9M/h (scale differs — that fleet had ~350 ships).
- **Finding (00:40, "right number before buying keepers again"):** the control is `keeperAutoApproveMinCash` (currently
  **3,000,000**, max 5,000,000) — below it every keeper probe asks the operator instead of auto-buying. The companion gate is
  `systemReleaseMinCash` (currently **100,000**), which is what let DJ26+FR76 stage/release with almost no float. A fresh
  system costs `freshKeepersPerSystem` 8 × ~30k ≈ **240k** (+jumps). The point of the number is not cash for its own sake: a
  release only pays the ~1.7x premium if enough trade hulls exist to run its routes (this week's systems ran 146-315/unit with
  too few hulls). **Recommendation: `keeperAutoApproveMinCash` 5,000,000 and `systemReleaseMinCash` 1,000,000** — at 5M the
  fleet can hold ~1.5M trader float, fund two fresh systems (~500k) and still buy 6-8 light haulers. Proposed, not yet applied.
- **Action (00:35, operator "Make those changes. We just have to stick to it"):** applied the keeper cash gate —
  `keeperAutoApproveMinCash` **3,000,000 → 5,000,000** and `systemReleaseMinCash` **100,000 → 1,000,000** (verified in
  `get_doctrine`). Effect: no automatic keeper probes below a 5M wallet, and staging a system can never leave the wallet under
  1M. The engine still lists "wanted a release" but has nothing staged and cannot stage below the gate. This is the number for
  the week; keepers wait until the trading fleet is capitalized.
- **Rule (00:45, operator):** no hull purchases — light haulers included — until the wallet holds **3M** (keepers still 5M,
  `keeperAutoApproveMinCash`). Manual `buy_ship` bypasses doctrine, so it is a by-hand discipline rule. **Escape clause
  (operator):** "unless we start struggling to get there" — if cash is flat/falling ~2h, the rate drops under ~80k/h, or
  traders sit idle, break the gate and buy a hauler to restart growth. Mirrored into `CLAUDE.md` ("Buying a ship").
- **Action (02:0x, operator "revert the switch"):** reverted the blunt global route-shape cap — `hauledHops` back to
  8/off and `positioningHops` back to 10. It was a fleet-wide limit that would also have refused 2-gate legs for the
  freighters we plan to buy.
- **Action:** wrote **`docs/slow-hull-local-routes-spec.md`** for the engineer agent — a per-speed route-shape clause
  (`slowHullSpeed` 15, `slowHullHauledHops` 1, `slowHullPositioningHops` 2), applied only to hulls at/below the speed
  threshold, with implementation points in `haulLimit.ts` / `dispatcher.ts` / `fleet.ts`, tests, acceptance criteria and
  a safe rollout (keys ship disabled).- **Action (03:4x, slow-hull clause enable→disable):** after deploying `dc39c5d` (build + `docker restart stcommand` on the box;
  `/version.json` loadedFileMtime 03:23:35Z; three keys present), enabled `slowHullSpeed=15`, `slowHullHauledHops=1`,
  `slowHullPositioningHops=2`. Observed loaded share 0.38 (was 0.35) and no "slow hull" refusals — the current board is all
  local or 1-gate (DJ26→AC33 and AC33→PT36 are 1 hop), so nothing was over the cap. **Operator: "switches off — we don't have
  any hulls over 15 right now so nothing will take long routes."** Disabled all three (values kept). The clause only helps a
  mixed fleet: turn it on when the first speed-36/60 freighter arrives, so it gates the speed-15 hulls and exempts the fast ones.
- **Rule (04:5x, operator):** keeper-probe approvals — **approve probes whose target market is a shipyard; never deny the rest.** Leave non-shipyard keeper cards pending for the operator to decide. (This corrects the earlier auto-deny while cash builds.) Context: 21 known shipyards, 11 covered, 10 not (AC33-A14E staged, AR37-DE3Z/ZA6A parked, BY84-DD3F parked, DJ26-CC4B pending, HA25-D21F/X14C/Z12D staged, VN83-BZ6B/CC8E staged). Shipyard keepers keep yard stock/prices fresh, which the nearest-yard buying rule depends on.

## Targets (operator, 2026-11-11 ~17:00Z) — growth ramp vs last week

Baseline: 10 traders (1 heavy THEO-64), 9 live systems, wallet 1.65M / wallet+holds 2.97M, rate ~312k/h.
Last week at the same point: ~27 live systems, 20-30 traders, 16M+ wallet. The gap is scale (capital + hulls +
live systems), not strategy. Plan: let cash build, then light haulers in batches; fund one staged system's keepers
alongside. Light haulers beat heavies on capacity per credit (80/315k ~0.25 vs 225/2.55M ~0.09).

  loaded share **>= 0.45**.
- **By 2026-11-13 06:00Z:** wallet+holds **>= 10M** (heavy/bulk line); traders **>= 22**; live systems **>= 25**.

3M hold and buy a hauler to restart growth. If ahead, keep reinvesting — do not let cash sit above ~4M unspent.

  (that clause is an AND on top of keepers, and it is off). `queueTours.ts` only sends tour ships. So this week's auto mode waited on keepers while THEO-4B had already priced YK92.

## 2026-11-12 04:15Z — built the tour-priced release clause (operator: "build the doctrine we need to automate this")
- **Answer to "does the code already send a tour ship to the next queued system?":** yes. `freshTours` (value = most ships, 2 here, enabled) runs `queueTours.ts` after each fresh-system check:
  free tour/explorer ships go to staged systems, nearest first, ready systems first, and stay until `releaseMinPricedPct` (80%) of the markets are priced. Its limits here: only 2 tour ships
  (THEO-4A, 4B), so YR4/CU32/F39 log "need a tour: no tour or explorer ship". What it did NOT do was release.
- **Built:** clause `releaseOnTourPct` (50%, default off). A staged system with a tour/explorer ship in it and at least that share priced is ready without 80% of its keepers; keeper-ready systems still go
  first; target, gap, holds, reachability unchanged. `src/engine/systemRelease.ts`, `doctrine.ts`, `fleet.ts` (fills `tourHere`), 5 tests in `tests/systemRelease.test.ts` (29 pass), CHANGELOG entry.
  Pushed to the feature branch and to `main` (f96fc0d); typecheck clean. The 3 failing `doctrine.test.ts` subtests fail identically on the parent commit (catalog counts, tenant default), not caused by this.
- **Not live yet:** the box deploys from `main`, so it needs a deploy/restart; then `set_doctrine releaseOnTourPct` 50 and enable. Until then releases are by hand (YK92 done 03:44).
- **Local test DB:** created role/db `claude_test` on the sandbox Postgres to run the DB tests (sandbox only).

## 2026-11-12 12:45Z — where the gate-open time is recorded
The engine logs a `gate_open` milestone in `action_events` (kind milestone, subject gate_open); the compare endpoint (`src/http/compare.ts`) and ops `analysis` (`summary.gateOpenHours`, `milestones`) read it. This week: home gate open at 2026-11-10 12:15:46Z, 47.2 h into the week (27 ships, 772,397 credits), about 3.4 h before last week's ~hour 50.6 first jump. ops `gate_history` only shows material readings, so it is the wrong tool for the open time. Hourly "Gate pace" line: take gate-open from ops analysis milestones.

I could not prove which applied; the log only prints the count, not the reason per ship. Worth adding a per-ship reason to the note.

## 2026-11-12 19:35Z — the neighbourhood idea, as the operator described it
Operator: a hub ("neighbourhood") is a big system plus the active systems around it; several run at once; ships stay inside their own; the engine then cannot hand out a crazy route across the galaxy. For next week. Written into docs/TODO.md item 9 (feature branch only, not on main). Evidence this week for sizing it: jumps cost 0.39-0.60M of every 2.4-3.0M hour in the 17:00-19:00 hours (55-87 jumps at about 6.9k each, about 15-20% of the hour's profit), each gate hop is followed by about a 9 minute cooldown, and the tour ships needed 1+ hour to walk to staged systems 5-10 hops out. Manual levers that exist today: `set_ship_reach system` (local-only ships; they get what cross-system ships leave) and the doctrine cap on jumps to a route's buy market (default 3, hard ceiling 10). Not done: grouping systems, a home per ship, a dispatcher bonus/penalty, per-neighbourhood tours and yards.

More lanes spread the keeper reads and give spares, but will not lower the box's action calls. Whether keeper rotation returns to about 7 min is the thing to watch at the 02:12 update; if it does not, the budget that throttles keepers (90 calls a minute in keeper_review) is a separate setting to look at.

## 15:30Z: keeper jump errors investigated (user: "dig into that"), plus a database disk-full warning

- **Cause found, harmless to trading:** the failing `keeper:jump` calls are new keeper probes hopping toward staged systems. Log lines like `THEO-395: hop to X1-AZ61 (toward X1-AZ61) failed, will retry: Ship action is still on cooldown for 172 second(s)` (also THEO-38B, 390, 39B, 39C, 39D, 1B0, 4B). In `hopToward()` (`src/engine/fleet.ts`, the catch after `jumpShip`) the cooldown error is logged and returns false, so the next tick tries again at once instead of waiting out the cooldown.
- **Pattern:** the error bursts follow each batch of probe purchases: 10:15 (82 errors in 15 min), 11:15 (93), 12:30-12:59 (84), 14:45-15:15 (168); quiet between. About 85% of keeper jump calls fail in a burst (e.g. 107 of 126 at 14:45). `keeper:ship` refreshes rose from about 3 to 13 a minute with them, so a burst wastes roughly 13 box calls a minute (about 14% of the 105 cap). No trade is blocked.
- **Fix not applied** (read-only investigation, no code change): written up as TODO item 9c (wait out the cooldown with `Pending` and `resumeAt`). The ship behind it was not one ship but every new probe in transit.
- **Separate and more serious: the Postgres disk was full at 15:01-15:02.** Log lines: `could not extend file "base/16385/180555": No space left on device` (survey of X1-AF66 after jump failed, three times), `state snapshot save failed ... base/16385/16804`, and `market transactions not saved ... base/16385/180600`. Writes succeeded again afterwards (ledger rows 15:22, role persists 15:23, keeper buys 15:26), so it was brief, but it means the database volume is at its limit. Lost: one state snapshot, one batch of market transactions, one system survey (retried). The log buffer only holds 240 min so earlier occurrences are not ruled out. Needs a look at the box's disk (Postgres data volume): free space, table sizes (ledger, market_snapshots, market_transactions, action_events), and old week data.
